### [QROC] Define: Anatomical position
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: Standing erect, head level and eyes looking forward, upper limbs hanging at sides with palms facing forward, thumbs pointing away from the body (laterally), feet parallel on the floor with toes pointing forward.
---
*Tags: Year 2022 | Exams, Final 2022*

---

### [QROC] Define: Fertilization
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: The complex process by which a haploid male gamete (spermatozoon) fuses with a haploid female gamete (mature oocyte) in the ampulla of the uterine tube to form a single diploid cell (zygote).
---
*Tags: Year 2022 | Exams, Final 2022*

---

### [QROC] Define: Spermiogenesis
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: The morphological transformation and differentiation of non-motile, rounded spermatids into mature, motile spermatozoa without further cell division (involves acrosome formation, nuclear condensation, neck/tail formation, and shedding of excess cytoplasm).
---
*Tags: Year 2022 | Exams, Final 2022*

---

### [QROC] Define: Edema
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: Abnormal accumulation of excess interstitial fluid in tissue spaces (caused by increased capillary hydrostatic pressure, decreased plasma oncotic pressure, increased capillary permeability, or lymphatic obstruction).
---
*Tags: Year 2022 | Exams, Final 2022*

---

### [QROC] Enumerate: The stages of development of the Notochord
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: 1. Notochordal process (cellular cord extending cranially from primitive node).
2. Notochordal canal (lumen forming within the process).
3. Notochordal plate (floor of canal fuses with underlying endoderm and degenerates).
4. Definitive notochord (plate folds longitudinally and detaches from endoderm to form a solid rod of cells).
---
*Tags: Year 2022 | Exams, Final 2022*

---

### [QROC] Enumerate: Types of protein channels
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: 1. Non-gated (leak) channels (e.g., K+ leak channels, always open).
2. Gated channels:
- Voltage-gated channels (opened by changes in membrane potential).
- Ligand-gated channels (opened by binding of chemical transmitters/ligands).
- Mechanically-gated channels (opened by physical deformation/stretch).
---
*Tags: Year 2022 | Exams, Final 2022*

---

### [QROC] Enumerate: Actions of the sympathetic nervous system on the skin
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: 1. Vasoconstriction of cutaneous blood vessels (via alpha-1 receptors).
2. Contraction of arrector pili muscles causing goosebumps/erection of hair (piloerection via alpha-1 receptors).
3. Secretion of sweat from eccrine sweat glands (sudomotor action via sympathetic cholinergic fibers / muscarinic receptors).
---
*Tags: Year 2022 | Exams, Final 2022*

---

### [QROC] Enumerate: Three types of isomerism in carbohydrates
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: 1. D and L isomerism (enantiomers / mirror images based on penultimate carbon).
2. Pyranose and Furanose ring structures (ring isomerism).
3. Anomerism (alpha and beta forms based on anomeric carbon C1 in aldoses or C2 in ketoses).
4. Epimerism (isomers differing in configuration around a single asymmetric carbon, e.g., glucose and galactose at C4, glucose and mannose at C2).
5. Aldose-Ketose isomerism (functional group isomerism, e.g., glucose and fructose).
---
*Tags: Year 2022 | Exams, Final 2022*

---

### [QROC] Enumerate: Types of connective tissue fibers
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: 1. Collagen (white) fibers (composed of collagen type I, provide high tensile strength).
2. Elastic (yellow) fibers (composed of elastin and fibrillin, provide elasticity and recoil).
3. Reticular fibers (composed of collagen type III, form delicate supportive meshwork in lymphoid and parenchymal organs).
---
*Tags: Year 2022 | Exams, Final 2022*

---

### [QROC] Enumerate: Non-membranous cell organelles
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: 1. Ribosomes (protein synthesis machinery).
2. Centrioles / Centrosome (microtubule organizing center and spindle formation).
3. Cytoskeleton (microtubules, microfilaments/actin, intermediate filaments).
4. Proteasomes.
---
*Tags: Year 2022 | Exams, Final 2022*

---

### [QROC] Enumerate: Types of glandular epithelium according to presence of ducts
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: 1. Exocrine glands (possess ducts that pour secretion onto surface or into lumen, e.g., salivary glands).
2. Endocrine glands (ductless glands that pour secretions/hormones directly into bloodstream, e.g., thyroid, pituitary).
3. Mixed (heterocrine) glands (possess both exocrine and endocrine parts, e.g., pancreas).
---
*Tags: Year 2022 | Exams, Final 2022*

---

### [QROC] Describe: The phases of fertilization
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: Phase 1: Penetration of the corona radiata by capacitated spermatozoa using hyaluronidase.
Phase 2: Penetration of the zona pellucida mediated by acrosomal enzymes (acrosin); triggers cortical/zona reaction to prevent polyspermy.
Phase 3: Fusion of the oocyte and sperm cell membranes; oocyte completes 2nd meiotic division forming female pronucleus; sperm head forms male pronucleus; pronuclei fuse to form diploid zygote.
---
*Tags: Year 2022 | Exams, Final 2022*

---

### [QROC] Describe: The types of synovial joints according to axes of movements
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: 1. Uniaxial joints (move in one axis/plane):
- Hinge joint (flexion/extension, e.g., elbow, interphalangeal joints).
- Pivot joint (rotation, e.g., atlantoaxial, superior radio-ulnar joint).
2. Biaxial joints (move in two axes at right angles):
- Ellipsoid / Condyloid joint (e.g., wrist joint).
- Saddle joint (e.g., 1st carpometacarpal joint of thumb).
3. Polyaxial / Multiaxial joints (move in three axes):
- Ball-and-socket joint (flexion/extension, abduction/adduction, rotation, e.g., shoulder, hip joints).
4. Non-axial (Plane) joints (gliding movement, e.g., intercarpal, acromioclavicular joints).
---
*Tags: Year 2022 | Exams, Final 2022*

---

### [QROC] Explain: The effect of parasympathetic stimulation on constrictor pupillae muscles, spleen, urinary bladder, and stomach
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: 1. Constrictor pupillae muscle: Causes pupilloconstriction (miosis) and accommodation for near vision.
2. Spleen: Parasympathetic has NO innervation or effect on the spleen capsule (spleen is exclusively innervated by sympathetic).
3. Urinary bladder: Causes contraction of detrusor muscle and relaxation of internal urethral sphincter, promoting micturition.
4. Stomach: Increases gastric motility (motor to stomach wall) and relaxes pyloric sphincter; increases secretion of gastric acid (HCl) and pepsin.
---
*Tags: Year 2022 | Exams, Final 2022*

---

### [QROC] Explain: Lecithin has a role in prevention of atherosclerosis
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: Lecithin (phosphatidylcholine) provides the fatty acid needed by the enzyme LCAT (Lecithin-Cholesterol Acyltransferase) to esterify free cholesterol in HDL particles (forming lysolecithin and cholesteryl esters). This esterification is crucial for reverse cholesterol transport, removing cholesterol from peripheral tissues and blood vessels and delivering it to the liver for excretion in bile, thereby preventing atheroma and plaque formation in blood vessels.
---
*Tags: Year 2022 | Exams, Final 2022*

---

### [QROC] Explain: Covalent modification can regulate enzyme activity (with example)
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: Enzyme activity is altered by reversible covalent attachment or removal of a chemical group (most commonly phosphorylation and dephosphorylation catalyzed by protein kinases and protein phosphatases).
Example: Glycogen phosphorylase is activated by phosphorylation (phosphorylase b converted to active phosphorylase a), whereas Glycogen synthase is inactivated by phosphorylation and activated by dephosphorylation.
---
*Tags: Year 2022 | Exams, Final 2022*

---

### [QROC] Explain: Regulation of lipolysis during fasting
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: During fasting, blood glucose drops, leading to decreased insulin secretion and increased glucagon and epinephrine secretion.
1. Glucagon and epinephrine bind to G-protein coupled receptors on adipocytes, activating adenylate cyclase.
2. Adenylate cyclase increases cAMP levels, which activates Protein Kinase A (PKA).
3. PKA phosphorylates and activates Hormone-Sensitive Lipase (HSL) and perilipin.
4. Active HSL hydrolyzes stored triacylglycerols into free fatty acids (FFAs) and glycerol, which are released into the circulation to supply energy to peripheral tissues and liver.
*Tags: Year 2022 | Exams, Final 2022*

### [QROC] Describe the general characteristics of epithelial tissue?
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: 1. Cells are closely aggregated with very little intercellular substance (scant ECM).
2. Avascular; receives nutrition and oxygen by diffusion from underlying connective tissue capillaries.
3. Rests on an underlying basement membrane that separates it from connective tissue.
4. Cells exhibit polarity (apical, lateral, and basal domains).
5. High regenerative capacity (frequent mitotic division).
6. Rich in sensory nerve endings.
---
*Tags: Year 2025 | Exams, Final 2025*

---

### [QROC] Mention three functions of glycoproteins?
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: 1. Structural components of cell membranes and extracellular matrix (e.g., collagen, fibronectin).
2. Cell surface recognition, antigenicity (e.g., ABO blood group antigens), and cell-to-cell adhesion.
3. Protective and lubricating functions (e.g., mucins of GIT and respiratory tracts).
4. Immune defense (immunoglobulins/antibodies).
---
*Tags: Year 2025 | Exams, Final 2025*

### [QROC] Enumerate body fluid compartments?
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: Total Body Water (TBW, 60% of body weight):
1. Intracellular Fluid (ICF): 2/3 of TBW (~40% of body weight).
2. Extracellular Fluid (ECF): 1/3 of TBW (~20% of body weight), subdivided into:
- Interstitial fluid (~15% of body weight).
- Plasma volume (~5% of body weight).
- Transcellular fluid (CSF, intraocular, synovial, pleural, peritoneal fluids, ~1-2%).
---
*Tags: Year 2025 | Exams, Final 2025*

---

### [QROC] Enumerate sympathetic V.D. (Vasodilator) fibers?
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: 1. Sympathetic cholinergic vasodilator fibers to skeletal muscle blood vessels (release ACh, active during stress/fight-or-flight anticipation).
2. Sympathetic vasodilator fibers to coronary blood vessels (via beta-2 adrenergic receptors or local metabolic vasodilation).
3. Sympathetic cholinergic fibers to sweat glands (causing release of bradykinin, which dilates cutaneous blood vessels).
4. Sympathetic fibers to salivary glands (release kallikrein -> bradykinin -> secondary vasodilation).
---
*Tags: Year 2025 | Exams, Final 2025*

---

### [QROC] Enumerate Functions of pelvic nerve (parasympathetic to pelvis)?
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: 1. Motor to the bladder wall (detrusor muscle) and inhibitory to the internal urethral sphincter -> promotes micturition.
2. Motor to the distal colon and rectum, inhibitory to internal anal sphincter -> promotes defecation.
3. Vasodilator to blood vessels of erectile tissue (corpora cavernosa) in external genitalia -> causes penile and clitoral erection (nervi erigentes).
4. Secretomotor to prostate, seminal vesicles, and pelvic glands.
---
*Tags: Year 2025 | Exams, Final 2025*

---

### [QROC] Explain the effect of parasympathetic stimulation on constrictor pupillae muscles, spleen, and stomach?
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: 1. Constrictor pupillae muscle: Stimulation causes contraction, leading to pupilloconstriction (miosis) and accommodation for near vision.
2. Spleen: Parasympathetic system has NO innervation to the spleen; hence it produces no effect on the spleen capsule or pulp.
3. Stomach: Increases gastric motility and tone (motor to muscular wall), relaxes the pyloric sphincter, and increases secretion of gastric juice (HCl, pepsinogen, and mucus).
---
*Tags: Year 2025 | Exams, Final 2025*

---

### [QROC] Explain the effect of sympathetic stimulation on the liver and bronchi & bronchioles?
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: 1. Liver: Stimulates glycogenolysis (breakdown of glycogen to glucose) and gluconeogenesis via beta-2 and alpha-1 adrenergic receptors, resulting in increased blood glucose level (hyperglycemia) to supply energy for stress/emergency.
2. Bronchi & bronchioles: Causes bronchodilatation (relaxation of bronchial smooth muscles via beta-2 adrenergic receptors) and reduces bronchial secretions, facilitating airflow during the fight-or-flight response.
---
*Tags: Year 2025 | Exams, Final 2025*

---

### [QROC] Explain the effect of inhibition of Na-K pump?
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: Inhibition of the Na+-K+ ATPase pump (e.g., by cardiac glycosides like digitalis / ouabain or hypoxia/ATP depletion) causes:
1. Intracellular accumulation of Na+ and loss of intracellular K+.
2. Decreased Na+ concentration gradient across the membrane, impairing secondary active transport mechanisms (e.g., Na+/Ca2+ antiporter, leading to increased intracellular Ca2+).
3. Gradual depolarization of the resting membrane potential (loss of electronegativity), leading to decreased cell excitability.
4. Cellular swelling due to osmotic influx of water following retained Na+.
---
*Tags: Year 2025 | Exams, Final 2025*

---

### [QROC] Compare between passive and active transport according to: (Concentration gradient, Energy, Carriers, Examples)?
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: 1. Concentration gradient: Passive transport occurs along (down) electrochemical gradient (from high to low); Active transport occurs against (uphill) electrochemical gradient (from low to high).
2. Energy: Passive transport requires no cellular metabolic energy (no ATP); Active transport requires metabolic energy (ATP directly in primary active transport or ion electrochemical gradient in secondary active transport).
3. Carriers: Passive transport may not require carrier (simple diffusion) or requires carrier (facilitated diffusion); Active transport always requires specific carrier protein (pump or transporter).
4. Examples: Passive: Simple diffusion of O2/CO2, Facilitated diffusion of glucose via GLUT-4; Active: Na+-K+ ATPase pump (primary), Na+-glucose cotransport / SGLT-1 (secondary).
---
*Tags: Year 2025 | Exams, Final 2025*

---

### [QROC] Compare between types of autonomic ganglia (Lateral, Collateral, Terminal) according to Site and Relay for?
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: 1. Lateral (Paravertebral) ganglia:
- Site: Arranged in two chains on both sides of the vertebral column.
- Relay for: Sympathetic preganglionic fibers destined for head, neck, thoracic viscera, body wall, and limbs.
2. Collateral (Prevertebral) ganglia:
- Site: In front of the vertebral column, around origin of main abdominal branches of the aorta (celiac, superior mesenteric, inferior mesenteric ganglia).
- Relay for: Sympathetic preganglionic fibers (splanchnic nerves) destined for abdominal and pelvic viscera.
3. Terminal (Peripheral) ganglia:
- Site: Close to or inside the wall of the effector organs (e.g., intramural ganglia of Meissner and Auerbach in GIT).
- Relay for: Parasympathetic preganglionic fibers (cranial and sacral outflow).
---
*Tags: Year 2025 | Exams, Final 2025*

---

### [QROC] Compare between primary and secondary cartilaginous joints as regarding to: (Type of cartilage, Ossification)?
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: 1. Type of cartilage:
- Primary cartilaginous (synchondrosis): Bones are joined by hyaline cartilage (e.g., epiphyseal plate, spheno-occipital joint).
- Secondary cartilaginous (symphysis): Articular surfaces covered by hyaline cartilage and connected by a plate of fibrocartilage (e.g., symphysis pubis, intervertebral discs).
2. Ossification:
- Primary cartilaginous: Temporary joints that completely ossify with age (synostosis) after bone growth ceases.
- Secondary cartilaginous: Permanent joints that do NOT ossify throughout life (except pathological ankylosis).
---
*Tags: Year 2025 | Exams, Final 2025*

### [QROC] Explain: The trachea has multiple C cartilaginous rings?
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: The trachea is supported by 16-20 C-shaped hyaline cartilaginous rings. The rigid cartilage keeps the tracheal lumen patent and prevents collapse during negative intrathoracic pressure of inspiration. The open posterior gap (closed by the smooth trachealis muscle) allows expansion of the adjacent esophagus during the passage of food boluses.

### [QROC] Describe different phases of fertilization?
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: Phase 1: Penetration of the corona radiata by capacitated spermatozoa using hyaluronidase.
Phase 2: Penetration of the zona pellucida mediated by acrosomal enzymes (acrosin); triggers cortical/zona reaction to prevent polyspermy.
Phase 3: Fusion of the oocyte and sperm cell membranes; oocyte completes 2nd meiotic division forming female pronucleus; sperm head forms male pronucleus; pronuclei fuse to form diploid zygote.
---
*Tags: Year 2025 | Exams, Final 2025*

### [QROC] Classify the bones according to their shapes and give one example for each type?
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: 1. Long bones: Length exceeds width, consist of shaft and two ends (e.g., Humerus, Femur).
2. Short bones: Cuboidal shape (e.g., Carpal bones, Tarsal bones).
3. Flat bones: Two plates of compact bone enclosing spongy bone / diploë (e.g., Scapula, Sternum, Parietal bone of skull).
4. Irregular bones: Complex irregular shape (e.g., Vertebrae, Hip bone).
5. Pneumatic bones: Contain air-filled cavities/sinuses (e.g., Maxilla, Frontal, Sphenoid bones).
6. Sesamoid bones: Embedded within tendons (e.g., Patella).
---
*Tags: Year 2025 | Exams, Final 2025*

### [QROC] Steps of fertilization:
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: Phase 1: Penetration of the corona radiata by capacitated spermatozoa using acrosomal hyaluronidase.
Phase 2: Penetration of the zona pellucida mediated by acrosin; triggers cortical and zona reaction to block polyspermy.
Phase 3: Fusion of oocyte and sperm cell membranes.
Phase 4: Oocyte completes its second meiotic division forming mature ovum and second polar body.
Phase 5: Formation and fusion of male and female pronuclei restoring diploid number (46) to form the zygote.

**Correct Answer**: -

2. Placenta accreta / increta / percreta: Abnormal deep invasion of chorionic villi into or through myometrium due to deficient decidua basalis.

### [QROC] Types of chorionic villi according to their structure and structure of each type:
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: 1. Primary chorionic villi: Core of cytotrophoblast cells covered by an outer layer of syncytiotrophoblast (formed at end of 2nd week).
2. Secondary chorionic villi: Extraembryonic mesoderm invades the center, forming a mesodermal core surrounded by cytotrophoblast and syncytiotrophoblast (early 3rd week).
3. Tertiary chorionic villi: Fetal blood capillaries and blood cells differentiate inside the mesodermal core (end of 3rd week); functional units for maternal-fetal exchange.

### [QROC] Parts of decidua and fate of each part:
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: 1. Decidua basalis: Lies deep to conceptus at implantation site; forms the maternal component of the definitive placenta.
2. Decidua capsularis: Covers the expanding conceptus facing uterine cavity; progressively stretches, atrophies, and fuses with decidua parietalis by 4th month.
3. Decidua parietalis: Lines the remainder of the uterine cavity; fuses with the chorion laeve/capsularis obliterating uterine cavity, and is shed as lochia after delivery.

### [QROC] The importance of Feed-forward mechanisms:
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: Feed-forward mechanisms anticipate changes in regulated physiological variables before they occur, triggering adaptive homeostatic responses in advance to minimize delays inherent in negative feedback loops.
Examples:
1. Shivering and cutaneous vasoconstriction upon entering a cold environment before core temperature drops.
2. Cephalic phase of gastric and salivary secretion triggered by the sight or smell of food before ingestion.
3. Anticipatory rise in heart rate and respiratory rate prior to commencing vigorous exercise.
---
*Tags: Year 2026 | Exams, Final 2026*

3. Metabolic water (water of oxidation produced by cellular metabolism of carbs, fats, proteins): ~200 - 300 mL/day.
Total daily water intake equals ~2100 - 2800 mL/day.

### [QROC] Total body water is lower in females than in males (Explain why):
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: Females naturally possess a higher percentage of adipose tissue (body fat) and a lower skeletal muscle mass compared to males. Adipose tissue is relatively anhydrous (contains only ~10% water), whereas muscle tissue contains ~75% water. Therefore, total body water accounts for ~50% of total body weight in females compared to ~60% in adult males.
---
*Tags: Year 2026 | Exams, Final 2026*

---

### [QROC] Why are parasympathetic actions more localized than sympathetic?:
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: 1. Restricted divergence: Parasympathetic preganglionic fibers synapse with very few postganglionic neurons (ratio ~1:2), whereas sympathetic fibers diverge widely (ratio 1:20 or more).
2. Location of ganglia: Parasympathetic ganglia are terminal (located in or immediately adjacent to target organs), confining responses to individual viscera.
3. Rapid transmitter degradation: Acetylcholine is rapidly hydrolyzed by local acetylcholinesterase, whereas sympathetic hormones (epinephrine/norepinephrine) enter systemic circulation and persist longer.
---
*Tags: Year 2026 | Exams, Final 2026*

---

### [QROC] Explain the effect of sympathetic stimulation on constrictor pupillae:
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: Sympathetic stimulation has NO innervation or direct effect on the constrictor pupillae muscle (which is innervated exclusively by parasympathetic postganglionic fibers via ciliary ganglion). Sympathetic fibers innervate only the dilator pupillae muscle to cause pupillodilatation (mydriasis).
---
*Tags: Year 2026 | Exams, Final 2026*

---

### [QROC] Explain the effect of vagus nerve on pancreas and gallbladder:
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: 1. Pancreas: Stimulates pancreatic acinar cells to secrete enzyme-rich pancreatic juice, and stimulates endocrine islets to release insulin and glucagon.
2. Gallbladder: Stimulates smooth muscle contraction of gallbladder wall and relaxes sphincter of Oddi, promoting discharge of bile into duodenum.
---
*Tags: Year 2026 | Exams, Final 2026*

---

### [QROC] Why does anterior hypothalamus stimulation decrease heart rate?:
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: The anterior hypothalamus (preoptic area) contains the heat-loss center and acts as a major parasympathetic center. Stimulation sends efferent descending projections to the dorsal motor nucleus of vagus and nucleus ambiguus in the medulla, increasing vagal cardiac efferent activity which releases acetylcholine at SA/AV nodes, opening K+ channels (ACh-sensitive) and causing hyperpolarization and bradycardia.
---
*Tags: Year 2026 | Exams, Final 2026*

### [QROC] Write the E.M. Structures of the Outer Mitochondrial Membrane?:
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: 1. Smooth, continuous trilaminar limiting membrane, ~6-7 nm thick, without folds.
2. Contains numerous voltage-dependent anion channels / porins (VDAC), making it freely permeable to ions and uncharged molecules up to 5 kDa.
3. Contains enzymes involved in mitochondrial lipid synthesis and fatty acid elongation (e.g., monoamine oxidase / MAO, acyl-CoA synthetase).
---
*Tags: Year 2026 | Exams, Final 2026*

---

### [QROC] What are the structural differences between cilia and microvilli?:
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: 1. Cytoskeletal core: Cilia contain an axoneme composed of microtubules in a '9+2' doublet arrangement; Microvilli contain a bundle of actin microfilaments linked by villin and fimbrin.
2. Basal structure: Cilia arise from basal bodies (centriole-derived 9 triplets); Microvilli insert into the terminal web of actin filaments in apical cytoplasm.
3. Function and Motility: Cilia are actively motile (ciliary beat for fluid transport); Microvilli are non-motile (increase apical absorptive surface area).
4. Dimensions: Cilia are long (5-10 μm) and thick (0.2 μm); Microvilli are short (1-2 μm) and thin (0.1 μm).
---
*Tags: Year 2026 | Exams, Final 2026*

### [QROC] Write the Electron Microscopic (E.M) Structures of the non-dividing nucleus?:
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: 1. Nuclear Envelope: Double membrane with outer membrane studded with ribosomes and inner membrane lined by nuclear lamina, enclosing perinuclear space and perforated by nuclear pore complexes.
2. Chromatin: Heterochromatin (condensed, electron-dense, inactive) and Euchromatin (dispersed, electron-lucent, active).
3. Nucleolus: Dense non-membranous body composed of fibrillar centers, dense fibrillar component, and granular component for rRNA synthesis and ribosome subunit assembly.
4. Nuclear Matrix (Nucleoplasm): Semi-fluid proteinaceous ground substance supporting nuclear contents.
---
*Tags: Year 2026 | Exams, Final 2026*

- **B)** Reaction due to carboxyl group
- **C)** Reaction due to both amino and carboxyl groups
- **D)** Reaction due to side chain
**Correct Answer**: A

### [QROC] Enumerate three functions of lecithin:
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: 1. Main constituent of pulmonary surfactant (dipalmitoyl lecithin / DPPC) that lowers alveolar surface tension and prevents atelectasis.
2. Major structural phospholipid of cellular membranes and circulating plasma lipoproteins (HDL, VLDL, chylomicrons).
3. Serves as essential fatty acid donor for LCAT (Lecithin-Cholesterol Acyltransferase) in HDL, promoting cholesterol esterification and reverse cholesterol transport to prevent atherosclerosis.
---
*Tags: Year 2026 | Exams, Final 2026*

---

### [QROC] Enumerate three Functions of glutathione?:
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: 1. Master intracellular antioxidant: Detoxifies hydrogen peroxide (H2O2) and lipid hydroperoxides via glutathione peroxidase.
2. Protects and maintains essential protein and enzyme sulfhydryl (-SH) groups in the reduced state, preventing erythrocyte hemolysis.
3. Phase II xenobiotic detoxification: Conjugates with electrophilic toxins, drugs, and carcinogens via glutathione S-transferase for urinary excretion (mercapturic acids).
---
*Tags: Year 2026 | Exams, Final 2026*

---

### [QROC] Enumerate three benefits of Free Radicals:
**Correct Answer**: -
**Explanation**: 1. Microbicidal defense: Phagocytic respiratory burst uses superoxide and hydroxyl radicals produced by NADPH oxidase to kill phagocytosed bacteria.
2. Physiological signaling: Nitric oxide (NO•) serves as an essential vasodilator, neurotransmitter, and regulator of vascular tone.
3. Antitumor surveillance: ROS induce apoptosis and programmed destruction of damaged, infected, or malignant cells, and mediate actions of cancer chemotherapy.
